# Stream1 Piece-Transformer Nsight Systems Profile - 2026-06-30

## Command

```bash
docker run --rm --gpus all -v D:\100XH100\.worktrees\stream1-piece-transformer:/workspace -w /workspace gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24 bash -lc "set -o pipefail; cmake -S . -B build-nsys-stream1 -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DBEAM_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=86 && cmake --build build-nsys-stream1 --target stream_benchmark -j2 && mkdir -p test_results && BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR=test_results/stream1_transformer_reference/weights_fp16 BEAM_STREAM_BENCH_REPORT=test_results/stream1_transformer_nsys_benchmark_2026-06-30.md nsys profile --trace=cuda,nvtx,osrt --stats=true --force-overwrite=true -o test_results/nsys_stream1_transformer_2026-06-30 ./build-nsys-stream1/stream_benchmark 0 2>&1 | tee test_results/nsys_stream1_transformer_2026-06-30.log"
```

## Environment

- GPU: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3070 Laptop GPU, compute capability 8.6, 8192 MiB
- Docker image: `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24`
- Nsight Systems: 2025.6.3.541
- Build: `build-nsys-stream1`, `BEAM_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=86`
- Weight dir: `test_results/stream1_transformer_reference/weights_fp16`

## Artifacts

- `test_results/nsys_stream1_transformer_2026-06-30.nsys-rep`
- `test_results/nsys_stream1_transformer_2026-06-30.sqlite`
- `test_results/nsys_stream1_transformer_2026-06-30.log`
- `test_results/stream1_transformer_nsys_benchmark_2026-06-30.md`

## Result

`stream_benchmark` completed successfully under Nsight Systems. The largest config, `B_MICRO=8192, concurrency=4`, was skipped by the benchmark memory guard because estimated scratch was `7702315008` bytes with `7419723776` free bytes.

Do not use the profiled `candidates/s` as a speed benchmark. Nsight statistics collection inflated timings heavily, especially for concurrent/big-scratch configs. Use this run for kernel mix, launch counts, and timeline structure.

## Nsight Kernel Summary

From `cuda_gpu_kern_sum`:

| Kernel group | GPU time share | Instances | Notes |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| CUTLASS GEMM kernels | 36.6% | 3349 | QKV, attention out, FF1, FF2, output projection |
| `stream1_transformer_attention51_kernel` | 34.8% | 788 | Still comparable to all GEMMs combined under Nsight |
| `stream1_transformer_layernorm_copy_kernel` | 10.4% | 1773 | Many unfused LayerNorm passes |
| `stream1_transformer_build_input_kernel` | 8.9% | 197 | Piece input construction remains materialized |
| `stream1_transformer_bias_silu_kernel` | 5.5% | 788 | Separate FF activation/bias kernel |
| `stream1_transformer_residual_bias_add_kernel` | 3.7% | 1576 | Separate residual/bias kernels |
| `stream1_transformer_cls_layernorm_kernel` | 0.1% | 197 | Output pooling norm is minor |
| `stream1_transformer_score_quantize_kernel` | ~0.0% | 197 | Score quantization is not a bottleneck |

NVTX summary showed `Stream1_transformer_inference_launch` with 197 instances and `Stream1_CUTLASS_linear_launch` with 3349 instances.

## Interpretation

The profile supports the current optimization direction: the remaining gap is not one small bug. The hot path is split between GEMM and attention, with visible overhead from many separate LayerNorm, SiLU, residual, and input-build kernels. To approach the PyTorch fast-path envelope, next work should focus on:

1. A better short-sequence attention backend or CUTLASS/CUTE-style fused attention for `seq_len=51`, `head_dim=32`.
2. Fusing `Linear + bias + SiLU` and residual/bias epilogues where possible.
3. Reducing LayerNorm launches, especially around the per-token pre-norm blocks.
4. Capturing a best-point-only Nsight profile after adding a benchmark filter, so profiler overhead from large/concurrent sweep points does not pollute the timeline.

Read-only inspection done. I would prioritize these:

1. **Fuse LayerNorm with its consumer path, not another standalone LN kernel.**  
   Target [cuda/stream1_transformer.cu](/D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/cuda/stream1_transformer.cu:1025): `stream1_transformer_layernorm_copy_launch` before QKV and FF1. The standalone LN alternatives in `test_results` were slower, but current LN still materializes `scratch.attention_context` only for the immediately following GEMM. A fixed `d_model=256` stats kernel plus transformer-only CUTLASS A-load transform would remove large LN write/read traffic.  
   Verify: `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, then Nsight must show fewer/cheaper `stream1_transformer_layernorm256_copy_kernel` passes without GEMM regression. Perf gate: Kaggle `512/1024 x concurrency=2` vs v9 `~474k`.

2. **Optimize attention layout while preserving tensor-core QK/PV.**  
   Target [cuda/stream1_transformer.cu](/D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/cuda/stream1_transformer.cu:869): QK -> `pack_v51` -> `softmax51` -> PV. Do not retry scalar warp attention or CUTLASS example FMHA. The safer candidate is to overlap or eliminate `stream1_transformer_pack_v51_kernel`: launch V-pack on an auxiliary stream immediately after QKV while QK runs, then sync before PV; or write V into PV-ready padded layout from the QKV epilogue if overlap does not help.  
   Verify: same correctness test; Nsight should show `pack_v51` overlapped or gone, with QK/PV still tensor-core kernels.

3. **Add transformer-shape-specific GEMM variants.**  
   Target [cuda/stream1_transformer.cu](/D:/100XH100/.worktrees/stream1-piece-transformer/cuda/stream1_transformer.cu:401), especially `linear_bias`, `ff1_linear_bias_silu`, and `linear_residual`. Current code uses one CUTLASS tile family across QKV `N=768`, FF1 `N=1024`, and residual/output `N=256`. GEMMs are the largest remaining bucket after FF1/QKV fusion, so per-projection template dispatch has better odds than broad 128x128 swapping.  
   Verify each projection variant independently with `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, then `stream_benchmark` filtered to `512x1`, `512x2`, `1024x2`, and Kaggle v-next. Keep a rollback threshold: any local win under ~3% probably will not survive T4 noise.

I would not spend next time on residual+bias broadcast epilogue, standalone LayerNorm variants, scalar/warp attention, or CUTLASS example FMHA; the notes show those were already slower or structurally the wrong direction. No files were edited.
